
# Known World — Step 2.1 / SW: Southern Westeros — Extended Source Research & Step 2.2 Integration, 298 AC

**Status:** Extended independently identifiable place/holding and environmental research; **NOT** pixel-by-pixel handwritten marker verification or completed Step 2.2 integration.  
**Scenario anchor:** `agot_298_ac`, **Royal Party Arrives at Winterfell, before the crypt conversation and Bran’s fall**. Relative event anchor, not a known canonical numbered day of 298 AC.  
**Source region:** `authoring/maps/Realms/sw.png` (2067 × 1894 pixel raster); adjacent `nw.png`.  
**Research updated:** 2026-10-17; reference state: `dtzudontsay/Minecraft_Server_Mod2`, `master`.  
**Explicitly pending:** user’s red-X detector observation file, false/missed/unreadable markings, original annotated-image pixel reading, on-the-ground world coordinates and scale-calibrated population. These are deferred by user, NOT implicitly solved.

## 0. What this dossier does and does not establish

The south-map source reaches the Riverlands, Vale, Westerlands, Iron Islands, Crownlands, Reach, Stormlands and Dorne. All **198 currently authored holding records** in these eight political domains (including eight regional roots) are individually inventoried below. The separate `SouthFeatureRegistry` defines **73 existing features** including rivers, roads, places and landmarks; all are recorded. This is **not** a claim that 198 different towns or 198 handwritten Xs exist. Several holdings share exactly one real-world island/city name, while large cities contain multiple districts and political institutions.

**Evidence legend.** `[C]` = published ASOIAF book, official atlas or historical source (source register); `[R]` = repository record verified as it exists, not independent book canon; `[I]` = geographically/historically plausible interpretation; `[M]` = explicitly proposed scenario estimate, **not** canon; `[U]` = not established; `[H]` = historic event/site, not automatically present-day occupancy; `[ADAPT]` = adaptation/fan/game-derived entry needing book-continuity review. General regional claims cite the source section and bibliography; each authoring record's house/ID is `[R]` unless separately sourced.

**One authoritative person invariant:** every human counts exactly once, whether materialized into a physical NPC or contained in an unexpanded **disjoint** cohort. A worker, farmer, soldier, mariner, servant, noble, visitor or captive is a role of a person already counted. Houses, holdings, jurisdictions, economic inventories and military units are **references to** such persons and property, not second databases inventing population. See `docs/architecture/20_3_POPULATION_AND_HOUSEHOLDS.md` and `20_3_ECONOMY_DOMAIN.md`.

**Chronology:** House Whent still holds Harrenhal; house Greyjoy is headed by **Balon as Lord Reaper under Robert**, not Euron as king; Hoster Tully is living though unwell; Robert Baratheon king; Lord Renly at Storm’s End; Lord Stannis on Dragonstone; Doran prince of Dorne; Tywin Lannister in west; Mace Tyrell in Reach. Jon Arryn has died; his son Robert Arryn inherits, with Lysa in the Vale. Tyrell marriage/war realignments, Joffrey's coronation, Baelish receiving Harrenhal, Tyrion's later offices, King's Landing starvation and sackings, Dornish Water Gardens residency as a *later-in-298* event all start **not-yet-applied** unless dated otherwise. [S02-S11,S16-S19]

**Population numbers:** not a real 298 AC census. No exact source headcounts are known for most holdings. Where ranges appear they are provisional [M] envelopes, should be calibrated **together** against productive area, crops, food consumption, labor and nearby settlement catchment. Absent values are `unknown`, never silent zero; no per-domain numbers are derived by multiplying `populationWeight`.

## 1. Eight political realms at the exact scenario start

| Realm (existing region location) | Primary government 298 AC [C] | Distinct legal/administrative issue | Primary economy/ecology | Current root holding ID [R] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Riverlands (`riverlands`) | Lord Hoster Tully at Riverrun; heir Edmure Tully | Big vassals often field more men than Tullys; frequent cross-realm invasions; twins tolls | Forks of Trident, farmed floodplains, barges, fishing, towns | `riverlands_domain` |
| Westerlands (`westerlands`) | Lord Tywin Lannister of Casterly Rock | Deep rock fortress distinct from Lannisport; mine rights are real work/property | Gold/silver extraction, farming lowlands, port trade | `westerlands_domain` |
| Iron Islands (`iron_islands`) | Lord Balon Greyjoy of Pyke, subject to Robert | Captains, captives/thralls, iron price and Drowned God institutions, not a uniform mainland manor | Fisheries dominate, iron mines, forge/ships, marginal arable land | `iron_islands_domain` |
| Crownlands (`crownlands`) | King Robert I Baratheon via Iron Throne, separate Stannis Dragonstone jurisdiction | Royal government vs City Watch, kingsguard, court property and crown vassals; naval domain | Capital food import, trades, royal dockyards, mixed coast and kingswood | `crownlands_domain` |
| Vale (`the_vale` / `vale`) | Young Lord Robert Arryn with mother Lysa as regent following Jon’s death | Mountain clans beyond effective ordinary lordly justice; Gates/Eyrie seasonal seats; same Gulltown city serves multiple family holdings | Protected fertile central valley, Gulltown sea trade, sheep and crops, mountain passes | `vale_domain` |
| Reach (`the_reach`) | Lord Mace Tyrell of Highgarden | Huge vassal strengths, Hightower city privileges, Citadel/Faith separate institutions, Redwyne navy | Most fertile agricultural realm, wine/vineyard and major ports/markets | `reach_domain` |
| Stormlands (`stormlands`) | Lord Renly Baratheon at Storm’s End; Robert king | Storm’s End has no safe harbor; Dornish Marcher rights and naval approach differ | Stormy coasts, rainwood timber, pasture, fishing and Marcher agriculture | `stormlands_domain` |
| Dorne (`dorne`) | Prince Doran Nymeros Martell at Sunspear | Distinct equal primogeniture, Rhoynar descendants, religious/cultural variation, orchards dependent on water | Dry interior, desert passes, Greenblood farms/rafts, Planky Town shipping | `dorne_domain` |

Important comparison: *lord's castle*, *region*, *port town*, *regional government*, and *formal organization* are separate entities. The `LandedHolding` type `CITY` or `TERRITORY` does not mean a fresh population or a new Minecraft city should be instantiated. South-map visual overlap with NW (Fingers, Neck, Twins) or any islands must reuse one permanent place identity.

These entries are **not observed `SW-NNN` detections**. They are historically/place-research-backed supplemental candidates to match against the source map and to connect to Step 2.2, with final selection from actual painted geography.


## 3. Region-by-region 298 AC profiles — governance, daily life and economic ecology

### 3.1 Riverlands — Trident corridor / politically fragile grain-and-water economy

**Government and family continuity [C].** Hoster Tully remains lord at Riverrun; his declining health matters to the future succession state, not current lawful replacement. Edmure is his heir, Catelyn Stark and Lysa Arryn are daughters linking North/Vale; Brynden Tully has a separate office and residence. Bannermen include Blackwood at Raventree, Bracken at Stone Hedge, Frey at Twins, Mallister at Seagard, Mooton at Maidenpool, Whent at Harrenhal, Piper at Pinkmaiden, Darry, Roote, Vance branches and others. Rival noble houses' ability to raise men is not a number of phantom troops. [S01,S14]

**Landforms and food [C/I].** Red/Blue/Green forks, the Tumblestone and God's Eye shape floodplains, fishing, barges, mills, orchards, market towns and roads. Canon explicitly notes rich farms, use of waterways to sell crops and frequent disruption by war. Proposed crop trials `[I]`: wheat, barley, rye, pulses, hay, vegetables, orchards and livestock, tested against alluvial fertile ground, flooding and season. Narrow riparian strips and oakwoods differ from expansive fields. Wheat-producing barge traffic needs real warehouses, draft animals, merchants, ferries and navigability by river section. [S01]

**Settlements and architecture.** Riverrun is a **triangular water-defended castle**, not a sprawling big city. The Twins has **two bridge forts** spanning a critical Green Fork crossing (House Frey). Harrenhal is giant and partially ruined yet held by Shella Whent: it requires a castle model with limited functioning precincts and enormous abandoned areas, not zero residents and not a fully serviced town. Maidenpool is an active market town/port (repo calls it `CITY`); Saltpans is a river-mouth coastal port, and `saltpans_cox_domain`/`saltpans_hawick_domain` must not cause two Saltpans to spawn. [S01,S18]

**Demography [M].** A populous mixed farming-and-market region; labor force concentrated in agriculture, fisheries, mills, carts, barges, market exchanges and castle service. Feudal status and faith (mainly Seven with Blackwood old-gods exception) assigned to individual people. Assign settlement-specific household count and age distribution, from real arable land and river food resources; keep a large unnamed-village hinterland. No troop mobilization at day zero from later battles.

**War/justice/trade.** Most natural traffic corridors expose farms to marching armies; future burning/siege damages actual fields, animals, bridges, residents and inventory. Old feuds (Blackwood–Bracken), former Hoare/Mudd/Strong royal eras and Robert's Rebellion provide historical memory and claims but not active instant combat.

### 3.2 Westerlands — Lannister rock, mining and coastal commerce

**Government [C].** Tywin Lannister controls Casterly Rock as Lord of the Westerlands; Tyrion, Jaime and Cersei have distinct life, legal and current-position histories (the last two are in Robert's northern visiting party at scenario start, not physically duplicated in King's Landing). Lannisport is administered through local Lannister branches/urban officers. Houses Lefford, Crakehall, Marbrand, Westerling, Farman, Kenning of Kayce, Prester, Swyft, Serrett, Brax and Lydden have distinct seats and lordships. [S02,S17]

**Industrial economy [C/I].** Gold and silver mines are genuine economic anchor, not infinite money; shaft depth, vein grade, miner hours, timber supports, fuel, carts, refining, wages, illness, labor casualties, transport and depleted veins should be future variables. Canon has gold at Casterly Rock, Golden Tooth and historic Castamere/Nunn's Deep/Pendric Hills, silver at Castamere. **Castamere is destroyed in 261 AC; no 298 AC working Reyne court**. Agricultural hinterland still required to support mines/port population. [S02]

**Landforms/cities [C].** Casterly Rock is a gigantic natural rock made into a castle; its full internal city-like spaces should not turn its `CASTLE` into a second Lannisport. Lannisport is a large active port, separate docks/quarters/farm markets. Golden Tooth guards the eastern river-road pass; Deep Den controls Goldroad; Faircastle on Fair Isle watches Ironborn sea-lanes; Kayce is a market/fortified seat; western sea cliffs/rolling fertile plains vary. [S02]

**Military [I/M].** Richly equipped castle guards, heavy cavalry/retinues, large levies requiring farm labor transfers, and coastal garrisons; pre-298 Lannister naval devastation in Greyjoy rebellion is past, not a permanent 298 fleet composition. Do not apply 50,000 semi-canon total mobilization to a standing army.

**Social/religion [C/I].** `westerman`, faith of Seven mainly, with urban diversity and local historical family memory of Reynes and Tarbecks. Mine workers are same identifiable residents who suffer injury, earn wage, live in households, produce real ore/ingots and may join armies.

### 3.3 Iron Islands — fisheries, maritime lordship, old law and iron mines

**Date-specific authority [C].** Balon Greyjoy of Pyke is a **lord subject to Robert** at our anchor. Rodrik and Maron, Balon's sons, died during 289 AC rebellion; Theon remains ward/hostage at Winterfell, not on Pyke. Asha's future claims, Euron's later kingship and kingsmoot are NOT initial status. Rodrik Harlaw the Reader controls Ten Towers, distinct from other Harlaw family estates; Victarion Greyjoy commands Iron Fleet later in narrative, verify actual 298 posting but do not create a new person at sea. [S03,S15]

**Economy with actual source facts [C].** Iron Islands have poor/thin soil, extensive rocky cliffs and fisheries. Archmaester Haereg reports roughly seven in ten families as fisherfolk historically—**source claim, not mandatory 298 household count**. Species include cod, black cod, monkfish, skate, sardines, mackerel, plus shellfish. Ore production including iron, tin/lead on several islands; Lordsport forged arms and armor. Every fishing boat requires fisher NPCs, catch/processing, preservation, and actual sale/household consumption. [S03]

**Legal status/social institutions [C/I].** Distinguish Drowned God priests, drowning/faith, reaving captains, ordinary families, landholders, thralls and salt wives. Raiding yields cargo through actual transfers and legal/captive status, not a free pirate tax. Do not set all Ironborn to pirates or raiders, nor assume thrall status for every miner. `ironborn` and `drowned_god` are existing reference IDs; individual and family exceptions allowed. [S03]

**Islands/geography.** Great Wyk (ore, Hammerhorn/Goodbrothers), Old Wyk (Drumm, Nagga's Hill sacred kingsmoot site), Harlaw (Ten Towers/Harlaw branches), Pyke (castle on separate sea stacks, Lordsport town on same island), Orkmont, Saltcliffe, Blacktyde and Lonely Light must be separate island landmasses. There are many **overlapping landed holdings** on Harlaw, Orkmont and Saltcliffe: do not instantiate several archipelagos or city populations from the duplicated worldLocationIds. [S03,S15]

**Military/ships.** Iron Fleet with actual vessels/crews, ordinary lordships' longships, ship maintenance and storm losses. Naval blockade is a flow of ships and provisions, not a percentage slider. A raid removes mariners from fishermen, farms, mines and families; captured goods are tracked.

### 3.4 Crownlands — urban capital, palace, naval approaches and feudal satellites

**Political structure [C].** King Robert's royal government holds capital and direct crownlands. House Baratheon has **three** administrative branches in this moment: Robert at King's Landing (physically away in North with royal visit), Stannis of Dragonstone, Renly of Storm's End. Stannis is not currently king and has not replaced the Faith with R'hllor statewide. Crownlands vassals include Velaryon, Celtigar, Bar Emmon, Massey, Buckwell, Rosby, Stokeworth, Rykker, Brune, Sunglass etc. [S05,S16]

**Capital as real ecology/economy [C/I].** King's Landing at mouth of Blackwater Rush is Westeros' most populous city. Distinguish Red Keep (royal palace), Great Sept of Baelor, guild/craft quarters, City Watch, walls and gates, docks/markets, Flea Bottom, hilltop residences and suburb slums. `red_keep_domain` is a child of `kings_landing_domain`: one urban census with identifiable palace employees, courtiers, militia/gold cloaks and maritime workers. High food imports, firewood, disease/sewage/contaminated water, class and property inequality must be simulated through actual stock/health/workplace relations. [S05]

**Satellites.** Duskendale is real port/market; Dun Fort is Rykker **castle**. Rosby/Stokeworth/Hayford are regional breadbasket/food providers, not simply names of castles. Crackclaw Peninsula wooded/isolated with Houses Brune and Crabb ruins; Massey's Hook coast with Stonedance, Sweetport Sound and Sharp Point. Dragonstone island is volcanic, Valyrian-style fortress and sea approach; Stannis's household, warships and commoners must not share one anonymous registry. Driftmark historically a powerful shipbuilding port, but avoid recreating the *historical* peak of Spicetown as a bustling 298 metropolis without chronology research. [S05]

**Institutions.** City Watch `gold_cloaks`, Kingsguard, royal court, City Watch command, Faith of Seven High Septon, royal justice/courts, merchant guilds and ship captains require organizations/offices. Their members are people and their property/treasury separate from King's personal wallet.

### 3.5 Vale — fertile protected valleys, seasonal mountain castles and separate maritime populations

**Dynastic chronology [C].** Jon Arryn died **before** exact scenario anchor; Robert Arryn is the underage Lord of the Vale and Lysa his mother/regent. Lordship and delegated office should be separate person records. Eyrie, Gates of the Moon, Bloody Gate are **three separate installations**; winter residence and mountain access change with season. Great vassals include Bronze Yohn Royce, Lady Anya Waynwood, Corbray, Grafton, Hunter, Redfort, Belmore, Lynderly, Templeton, Coldwater; not all members of one Arryn household. [S04]

**Climate and food [C/I].** The Vale proper is notably fertile, with wheat, corn, barley, pumpkins and fruit recorded. High mountains and valleys are comparatively isolated; clans may raid the high road and their leaders are not conventional feudal vassals. The Fingers have rocky maritime smallholding terrain and little great farming; Three Sisters belong to Vale and are already covered by NW, one place identity across images. [S04]

**Transport and construction.** High Road → Bloody Gate → fertile Vale → Gates of the Moon → Eyrie via arduous steep passage. Gulltown is Vale's urban trading port, with Grafton control and separate Arryn/Shett family rights: do NOT generate three Gulltown cities from three `CITY` holding records. Maritime trade and river roads, mountain passes and snow-closing season profoundly affect military response/food supply. [S04]

**Culture/legal [C/I].** `valeman` dominant in Vale proper, regional `vale_mountain_clan` distinct local mountain communities, `sisterman` maritime exception; Faith of Seven is broadly influential. Religion and loyalties individual; the Lords Declarant belong to **later** political events, no start-day completed coalition.

### 3.6 Reach — primary food producer, old towns and religious/academic autonomy

**Feudal structure [C].** Lord Mace Tyrell of Highgarden, heir Willas. Houses Hightower (Oldtown), Redwyne (Arbor), Tarly (Horn Hill), Rowan (Goldengrove), Florent (Brightwater Keep), Oakheart (Old Oak), Caswell (Bitterbridge), Footly (Tumbleton), Chester/Serry/Hewett (Shield Islands), Fossoway branches and more hold nested rights. Highgarden was a former Gardener royal seat; *House Gardener is historical, not a living ruling dynasty*. Later Brightwater forfeiture or Tyrell grants are not day-zero. [S06,S07,S08]

**Fertility [C/I].** Source explicitly identifies the Reach as most fertile and heavily populated region. Mander valley farms, tributaries Blueburn/Cockleswent, orchards (melons, fireplums, peaches, apples, grapes source-attested at regional scale), fertile fields, trade/irrigation, vineyards and grazing. Wide farmed plains differ from wooded hills, Dornish Marches, Honeywine river and rocky island coasts. Work cycles need variable crop/fruit seasons; `agricultural_capacity.json` Reach average alone cannot model citrus/wine/honey/hops/fruit processing. [S06]

**Oldtown's special status [C].** Major city at Honeywine/Whispering Sound mouth, second population after King's Landing and exceptionally prosperous. Hightower on Battle Isle, Citadel's maester organization, Starry Sept, city watch, faiths, banks and foreign merchant districts. Existing scenario has `hightower_domain` fortress but **no separate Oldtown city holding**. Thus 2.2 must establish one `oldtown` settlement plus tower/citadel/sept subassets, not merely spawn the Hightower as a farm castle and omit the city. [S07]

**Naval trade [C].** Arbor is a grape/wine island with three significant ports, and Redwyne fleet under Lord Paxter: approximately **200 warships and five times as many merchant-type ships** is source-backed capacity summary, not 1,200 ships guaranteed simultaneously docked/active on day zero or 1,200 newly generated crew complements. Shipyard wood demand, docks, port tariffs, cargo wine casks and sailor households follow single inventories. [S09]

**Transportation.** Roseroad links Oldtown–Highgarden–King's Landing; Ocean Road joins Highgarden toward Lannisport; Mander navigability and river barges distribute food. Internal towns and inns must serve real routes. Fall of the Mander trade can starve city population even when farms remain fertile, so economic geography drives politics.

### 3.7 Stormlands — storm-protected forts, rainwood and militarized passes

**Authority [C].** Renly Baratheon holds Storm's End. Lord Edric Storm is Robert's bastard ward at Storm's End in the general book period; chronology/initial residence check. Ser Cortnay Penrose is castellan, not a royal prince. Beric Dondarrion's later Brotherhood does not exist on day zero. Marcher families Swann, Caron, Selmy, Dondarrion and others have border obligations; Dornish antagonism is historical political context, not automatically ongoing open war. [S10]

**Coastal geography [C].** Storm's End has massive curving wall and drum tower, no safe anchorage directly below, facing notoriously violent Shipbreaker Bay. Cape Wrath includes rainwood and older coastal warning-tower village networks, prosperous Weeping Town, islands Tarth/Estermont, and rainy but farmable lands. A naive generic castle waterfront would be wrong. [S10]

**Economy/climate [I].** Rainwood timber, fuel and charcoal, coastal fisheries, storm-damaged shipping, rainy fields, orchard opportunities, livestock, Marcher grain/pasture, quarry and crafts. Ship repair demand high at safe harbors and ports (especially Weeping Town), but no permanent all-region monsoon tax. Different microzones: rain forest, windy grassy Marches, Red Mountain passes and Tarth sapphire-blue water (color name does not prove productive sapphire mines). [S10]

**Military/travel.** Dornish Marches hold castles in prepared terrain; passes/roads require supply depots and mounted patrols. Fleets use actual coastal harbors, not castle curtain walls. Strong storms shut transport and may wreck ships, with persistent crew and cargo loss.

### 3.8 Dorne — three societies, desert interior, irrigated valleys and separate Martell urban forms

**Government/legal [C].** Prince Doran Nymeros Martell rules, with heir Arianne according to equal primogeniture. The Rhoynish legacy includes women's succession rights and diverse local traditions; `stony_dornish`, `sandy_dornish`, `salty_dornish`, `orphan_greenblood` IDs already exist and should influence generated person culture only as evidence-informed priors. Yronwood is a powerful former-rival house, alongside Dayne, Fowler, Allyrion, Uller, Wyl, Vaith and others. No blanket non-Dornish succession law and no invented unified foreign-style monarchy in scenario. [S11]

**Geography [C].** Red Mountains and their Prince's Pass/Boneway separate the northern frontier; arid interior/red and white sands, stony coasts; eastern Dorne drier and less fertile, river and some western valleys more irrigable. The Greenblood from Vaith+Scourge at Godsgrace sustains miles of farms, orchards and canals (lemons, olives source-supported along river). [S11]

**Distinct urban/river sites [C].** Sunspear castle/Old Palace, Spear Tower and Tower of the Sun; Shadow City west of palace, a labyrinth of mudbrick shops and streets; **Planky Town** at river mouth, largest settlement of Dorne, a *floating town* of barges lashed together rather than ordinary stone streets. Water Gardens separate Martell retreat with children, pools and citrus; Prince Doran's movement there during 298 must be stored in later chronology rather than assumed before the royal-visit anchor. [S11]

**Agriculture [C/I].** Whole-Dorne numeric farmland averages are insufficient. Oasis wells, terraces, irrigation canals, river valley fruit and olives/lemons, desert herding and fisheries are distinct. Water withdrawals, ownership, river low flow, pack animals, caravan water capacity and storage must gate production. No high-yield wheat farms on bare sands just because they are inside a political `dorne` polygon.

**Maritime/road connection.** Ports around Greenblood mouth and Dornish coasts, river rafts, Red Mountains passes, Sea of Dorne, Stepstones proximity. Shipping to Oldtown/Free Cities and local rafts use actual crews/cargo. Different house claims, kin relations and years of prior Martell–Targaryen cooperation shape political motivations, but later revenge plots are not predetermined.


## 4. Environmental/territorial zone dossier — raster-defined SW overlay and physical generation

The **actual** `reference/subregion_masks.json` defines `SW` at **2067×1894** with the following 31 colored subregion entries (plus surrounding `territory_zones.json` polygons for the eight realms). The color is an **existing raster lookup**, not a political ownership declaration. A broad `iron_islands` territory polygon must clip to real land, while `cape_kraken` and `the_neck` are NW/SW map overlap. In the table, land-use assumptions are [I] unless otherwise source cited; fine-scale crop area/probabilities remain unknown before raster/elevation calibration.

| the_neck | #4D119C | Swamp and water channels, crannogmen [C], wetland fish/reeds [I] | NW duplicate, no temperate farm blanket |
| cape_kraken | #BA1CAB | Northern western rocky cape and exposed fishing coast [I] | NW duplicate; don't give to Iron Isles |

| Vale central farming basin | Vale | Source-attested grain/fruit crops with fertile valley soil, mountain storm/snow constraints |
| Vale mountain clan territory | Vale | Non-feudal society, seasonal grazing, distinct clan organization and kin networks |
| Stepstones fringe | Off-map near Dorne/Stormlands | Sea route reference only; don't assign Stepstones to Dorne or copy SE Essos settlements |

### 4.2 Specific source-mask ID issues to resolve before code

1. `subregion_masks.json` uses `gray_shield` whereas `SouthFeatureRegistry.java` uses `grey_shield` / "Grey Shield" and island aliases may vary in other references. Decide one canonical `WorldLocationId`/`geographicFeatureId`, preserve aliases and migrate raster lookup without creating two Greyshield islands.
2. `subregion_masks.json` uses `the_prince_pass` while `SouthFeatureRegistry` registers `the_princes_pass` ("The Prince's Pass"). Treat as one pass; validate whether each ID exists in `reference/locations.json`. A missing location lookup could silently drop the raster identification.
3. SW overlay also includes `the_neck`, `cape_kraken`, `the_fingers`; these are NW/SW overlap and cannot create duplicate landmasses, rulers, residents, roads or military installations.
4. `SouthFeatureRegistry` registers `the_reach` as a region, while the Reach `the_reach` parent overlaps its various island masks. Island specificity and sea-clip must win over broad realm background.
5. `the_twins` is owned by riverlands holding though North feature registry includes it; do not put it under Stark domain.
6. River/bay/cape geometry types and world-coordinate lookup must be independent of political polygon; a map color is not a new household or workplace.


## 5. Every existing SW political holding: 198 individual Step 2.1 research-to-data dossiers

The following record IDs `SW-H001` ... `SW-H198` are **research index numbers**, **not** claim of painted red-X ID or actual source-map marker order. Each entry repeats the **exact current GitHub `LandedHolding.authoredId`**, holding type and `ownerDynastyId`; site-specific detail has been checked against region lore or explicitly marked as preliminary/repo-only. Major districts/ports without land-holding records are instead in §2; do not miss them because they lack a holding record.

**Read population envelopes correctly:** `CASTLE` headcounts describe residents **physically in/around the castle enclosure**, not everybody on the house's farms or in the entire domain. Separate rural villages must be surveyed/generation-planned and cannot also count as castle residents. `TERRITORY`/`REGION` do not mint a second aggregated population. War levies, guards and navies are selected from living residents and traveling people, never additional population. These model estimates are **not canon** and are not executable starting census figures.

**Known-holder convention:** only named incumbent where 298 AC name is reasonably sourced; even there, an actual runtime `holderNpcId` must be a separately verified existing/created persistent person record, **never auto-generated from string**. Most current scenario holdings have `holderNpcId = null` even when `ownerDynastyId` exists.

## 6. Complete crosswalk: the existing 73 `SouthFeatureRegistry` names

These are *geographic features*, NOT 73 separate inhabited sites. Some features (islands, waterways, roads) overlap the 198 holding associations, and some roads are shared with NW's `kingsroad`. ## 7. Numeric and demographic modeling: regional planning **priors** and conservation

### 7.1 Relative productive capacity (not actual current hectares/census)

These are **relative environmental tuning priors** on 0–1 scale **by favorable land parcel** rather than averaged over an entire political region. For example, even very arable Dornish Greenblood fields are a small share of dry Dorne. Values are `[M]` and must be tested against terrain, land use and households. `HoldingAgricultureCatalog` currently has only The North, Reach, Dorne regional profiles, not full eight SW differentiated agriculture. Creating an economic crop from these heuristic values would be unsafe.

| Fine terrain group | Grain arability [M] | Orchard/vine [M] | Pasture [M] | Wood [M] | Fish [M] | Binding constraint |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Trident floodplain | .60–.90 | .30–.70 | .50–.80 | .15–.50 | .50–.80 | floods, navigability, armies |
| Riverland wooded upland | .25–.65 | .25–.60 | .25–.60 | .50–.85 | river pockets | forest clearance |
| Westerland fertile coastal lowland | .40–.75 | .35–.70 | .30–.75 | .20–.60 | coast .55–.90 | mine work competes for labor |
| Westerland mineral hills | .05–.30 | .05–.35 | .15–.45 | .15–.60 | low | rock/slope, fuel, ore transport |
| Iron Isles rocky uplands | .02–.25 | .02–.25 | .20–.55 | .05–.30 | coast .65–.95 | thin soil, storms, marine food |
| Crownlands capital hinterland | .40–.75 | .25–.60 | .30–.70 | .15–.45 | coast .40–.80 | urban demand and property |
| Vale inner valleys | .65–.95 | .45–.85 | .45–.85 | .25–.60 | rivers | sheltered fertility, gate access |
| Vale highlands/rocky Fingers | .03–.30 | .10–.35 | .20–.70 | .20–.65 | coast if near | snow, isolation, clans |
| Reach Mander lowland | .75–.98 | .55–.95 | .55–.85 | .20–.55 | river .40–.80 | land tenure, market transport |
| Reach Arbor/vine islands | .30–.70 | .75–.95 | .15–.55 | .20–.55 | coast .50–.85 | grape/vine specialization |
| Stormlands rainwood | .15–.45 | .10–.40 | .20–.55 | .75–.98 | coast variable | forest, wet soils, storm damage |
| Stormlands Marcher plain | .40–.75 | .20–.55 | .45–.90 | .05–.35 | low | border defense and weather |
| Dornish desert | .00–.08 | .00–.12 | .01–.30 | .00–.10 | unknown | **no water → no ordinary farms** |
| Dornish Greenblood irrigated land | .55–.92 | .65–.95 | .25–.65 | .20–.45 | river .50–.85 | water rights, canal maintenance |
| Dornish Red Mountains | .02–.25 | .05–.40 | .10–.65 | .05–.40 | streams | slope and pass constraints |

### 7.2 Working-adult employment compositions [M] — sum to 100% each

The denominator here is **economically active adults**, not all human residents, and a worker can fill only labor-hour-limited roles. For salaried soldiers, care must be taken not to add their headcounts again as ``extra`` residents. `Food` includes farmers/herders/fishers; `Industry` includes craft/mining/shipyards/processing; `Trade` includes carts/ports/barges/markets; `Services` includes domestic/administrative/religion; `Security` includes salaried watch/retainers and funded troops. A regent or noble household may be a **status/office**, not a separate full-time occupation.

| Settlement/economy archetype | Food | Industry | Trade/transport | Services/admin/faith | Security | Sum |

| Riverland river-port town | 26 | 20 | 31 | 16 | 7 | 100 |
| Westerlands mining village | 32 | 37 | 10 | 16 | 5 | 100 |

*Important:* Regional workforce percentages inherited from the previous dossiers can serve as **seed priors** but MUST be corrected with actual town vs countryside resident ratios, age/class structure, agricultural land, labor calendars and household-level demand. They are not static quotas to satisfy with duplicate NPCs.

### 7.3 City/castle population planning: bounded and not authoritative

| Settlement and footprint | Permanent census tentative range [M] | Source caveat and model rule |
|---|---:|---|
| King's Landing **city and contiguous slum/districts** | 280,000–550,000 | Often a roughly half-million magnitude in later storyline; day-zero headcount unverified. Red Keep, Street of Steel, Flea Bottom already included. |
| Oldtown **city** | 150,000–350,000 | Canon populous and rich but no fixed 298 census; Citadel and Hightower employee households included. |
| Lannisport **city** | 45,000–140,000 | One port census; do not add a city-sized population under Casterly Rock again. |
| Gulltown **one city** | 15,000–65,000 | Three named Gulltown holding records are political/cadet claims, not 3 urban populations. |
| Maidenpool **town** | 3,000–12,000 | `CITY` data type is not proof of great-city status. |
| Duskendale **town/port** | 2,500–12,000 | Dun Fort residents separate but neither body counted twice. |
| Planky Town floating built-up district | 2,500–12,000 | Source largest settlement in Dorne; should exceed Shadow City in same calibration. |
| Shadow City of Sunspear | 1,000–8,000 | Must stay below Planky Town; Sunspear palace is adjacent. |
| Weeping Town | 800–6,000 | Stormlands regional port, not another King's Landing. |
| Lord Harroway's Town | 700–5,000 | Trident town + farm domain separately counted. |
| Typical castle precinct | 35–350 | Excludes rural lordly domain and garrison counted as subset. |
| Protected ancient RUIN (e.g., Oldstones, Castamere) | 0 **established ordinary urban residents** | 0 does not rule out actual hermits/outlaws/explorers, but no default city census or production. |

**Incompatibility warning:** Sampling every row's upper bound independently and adding it to each attached holding will massively overcount Westeros. Step 2.2 must produce one coherent aggregate world-population constraint; reconcile migration, urban food imports, farming area, births/deaths and labor availability before exact count allocation. Population may be represented efficiently, but a physical NPC must always materialize from the same authoritative person record/cohort reservation.


## 8. Step 2.2 authoritative implementation contract — SW cannot simply become a standalone JSON town list

This contract expands `KNOWN_WORLD_STEP_2_1_COVERAGE_AND_2_2_HANDOFF.md` and should be used **before editing any code**. Where architecture already exists, migrate/extend the owner rather than implement another manager. The following are **field design proposals**, not existing code or current-schema promises.

### 8.1 Entity and foreign-key relationships

```
sourceMapObservationId (SW-NNN: user original, unresolved now)
   ├── sourceImageSha, label, rawRegionalPixel, userCorrectionNote
   └── candidatePlaceId (SW-Hnnn is NOT the observation ID)
              │
              ▼
CanonicalWorldLocationId / GeographicFeatureId
   ├── aliases / historical names / source provenance / 298 status
   ├── landmass + terrain-specific raster zone + political polygon
   ├── physical anchor + settlement/ruin/river/building footprint [later]
   ├── zero-or-many LandedHoldingIds; many-to-one same city allowed
   │       ├── ownerDynastyId, deJureDynastyId, parentHoldingId
   │       ├── current holderNpcId, titleId, governmentOrganizationId
   │       └── legally/physically separated estates, shipyards, shops, farms
   └── zero-or-many **distinct settlements / wards / villages / camps**
           ├── unique resident census of people or disjoint cohorts
           ├── household IDs, dwellings, family relations, legal status
           ├── daily job and workplace time allocation (same people)
           ├── farmable land, crop plots, animals, irrigation, fishery
           ├── real inventories and production/consumption/transaction log
           ├── port/ship/road/market network with crews and capacities
           ├── stationed watch and field levies (subset of people)
           ├── personal religion/culture/profile/beliefs/memories
           └── building generator footprints and player NPC projections
```

#### Recommended schema fields for the researcher/authoring join

`researchId`, `observationIds[]`, `mapId`, `sourceSha`, `rawImageX`, `rawImageY`, `rawLabel`, `interpretationName`, `canonicalName`, `aliases[]`, `confidence`, `primarySource[]`, `sourceYear`, `historicalOrCurrent`, `startYear`, `endYear`, `worldLocationId`, `featureId`, `landmassId`, `territoryId`, `regionalZoneId`, `locationParentId`, `coastalOrInland`, `terrainHeight`, `isLand`, `masterPixelX`, `masterPixelY`, `minecraftX`, `minecraftZ`, `structureFootprint`, `siteKind`, `siteStatusAt298`, `settlementIds[]`, `holdingIds[]`, `physicalSiteParentId`, `rulerNpcId`, `deJureDynastyId`, `ownerDynastyId`, `liegeDynastyId`, `organizationId`, `titleId`, `legalCodeId`, `populationTarget`, `populationConfidence`, `censusResidenceScope`, `householdTypePriors`, `demographicPriors`, `cultureWeights`, `religionWeights`, `classWeights`, `employedAdultJobWeights`, `workerSource`, `militaryPostingPriors`, `musterAvailability`, `shipCrewRoles`, `pastureHectares`, `farmableHectares`, `cultivatedHectares`, `irrigationNetwork`, `soilGroup`, `grainCropSuitability`, `orchardSuitability`, `fisheryAccess`, `minerals`, `woodlandExtraction`, `winterReserve`, `tradeRoutes[]`, `roadGateIds[]`, `riverSegmentIds[]`, `portId`, `buildingArchetype`, `iconicLandmarkIds[]`, `sourceConflicts[]`, `reviewNeeded`, `errorCategory`, `implementationOwner`, `importVersion`.

All unknown values are `null`/UNKNOWN; do not use `0` for an unknown army or settlement. `SW-Hnnn` identifies a *research dossier*, not a project `HoldingId` or Minecraft position.

### 8.2 Spatial, physical map and worldgen systems

Existing owners: `MapZoneRegistry`, `RegionalMapTransform`, `WorldReferenceCatalog`, `RegionalSubregionResolver`, `TerritoryZoneResolver`, `LandmassZoneResolver`, `KnownWorldGeoSampler`, `KnownWorldChunkGenerator` and biome/elevation data.

1. Preserve source image pixel coordinates even when they are later corrected. Resolve `SW` into master coordinates by the **calibrated affine transform**, not a simple rectangular scaling. Confirm Minecraft 1 block = 1 horizontal metre consistently.
2. Map broad political territory polygons separately from habitat/fertility layers; castle centers and village footprints must snap onto valid **land** with slope, coast, river and elevation constraints. Islands cannot contain the same sea/land cell twice.
3. Existing `reference/subregion_masks.json` SW has several **typo/alias mismatches** (`gray_shield` and `the_prince_pass`). Validate every RGB lookup against a real `WorldLocationId`, then unify duplicate feature aliases.
4. `SouthFeatureRegistry` 73 names plus `NorthFeatureRegistry` overlap: deduplicate `kingsroad`, the Neck, Twins and islands appropriately. River geometries remain continuous line networks, not many separate dots.
5. Give iconic sites (Casterly Rock, Eyrie, Harrenhal, King's Landing, Oldtown, Planky Town, Highgarden, Storm's End, Sunspear, Pyke, etc.) **special archetypes**. Do not generate anonymous vanilla villages inside walls or generic fortress cliffs.
6. Terrain constraints connect economy: vineyards on suitable Arbor soils, Vale grain on valley floor, Greenblood irrigated strips, iron mines on source-backed hill patches, rainwood timber, rugged island fisheries. Do not overwrite pre-authored mountains with vanilla random elevations.
7. All `SW-H` physical placeholders keep `sourcePixel/worldPosition=null` until detector/user verification and actual map calibration. A political holding can simulate abstractly before a permanent structure is built.

### 8.3 House, titles, court and administration

Existing owners: `LandedHoldingManager`, `DynastyManager`, `DynastyScenarioBootstrapService`, `DynastyInheritance`, `TitleManager`, `ClaimManager`, `SuccessionService`, `AuthorityService`, `OrganizationManager`, `NonDynasticSocietyManager`, `DynastyRelationshipManager` and scenario `dynasty_hierarchy.json`.

1. Validate 198 holding IDs and owners exactly; roots count 20 worldwide, but eight belong to SW. Validate `parentHoldingId` DAG, and de jure vs de facto owners separately. Houses on same island/port may have multiple independent holdings tied to one `WorldLocationId`.
2. Use existing special dynasty hierarchy overrides: Harlaw cadet families under main Harlaw, smaller Vale Sister houses under Sunderland, Frey vassals Charlton/Erenford/Haigh, Wode under Whent, Goldengrove/Reach small houses as known. **Do not** default all minor political influences to lord paramount if secondary evidence supports an intermediate liege.
3. Keep lord/seat/office separate: Stannis' Dragonstone vs Robert's crown domain vs Renly's Storm's End; Hightower city vs Citadel corporate institution; Arryn underage lord vs Lysa regency; Prince Doran's office and personal home.
4. Maintain culture-specific legal rules: Dornish succession **not** uniform male-preference primogeniture, Ironborn captives/Old Way distinct from crownland law, Vale clans outside full ordinary valley lord system, merchant houses and monasteries not all landholding dynasties.
5. Preserve historical-house extinct/ruin records (Reyne and Castamere, Tarbeck, Hoare/Mudd, extinct Gardener kings) as history and claims, not populated active 298 lords.
6. Honor exact scenario: Jon Arryn dead; Robert Arryn alive; Robert Baratheon physically at Winterfell as part of *existing royal court population pack*; Cersei/Jaime/Tyrion similarly not simultaneously in King's Landing.
7. All feudal obligations/tolls/levies/taxes must translate into **actual economic transfer or service**, not create goods or soldiers through abstract strength scores.
8. Add a review for `The Whispers`: catalog `status=ACTIVE`, but source depicts ruins/old Crabb site; legal active holding and **physical ruined structure** must be representable separately rather than rewriting history. Check `Standfast` current standing and `Riverspring` adaptation provenance.
9. `saltpans_cox_domain` and `saltpans_hawick_domain` are **both** `status=ACTIVE`, `type=TOWN`, `worldLocationId=saltpans`. Source of each family's jurisdiction is not verified. Hold them as unresolved overlapping legal claims, **not** proof both houses own independently functioning townships. Similarly the three Gulltown `CITY` entries require one city population and localized rights.

### 8.4 Population conservation, NPC materialization and households

Existing design: `NpcRegistry`, `NpcIdentity/NpcState`, `CharacterProfile`, `CharacterLegalStateManager`, genealogies, marriage/parentage, `PopulationPackCatalog`, planned household/cohort architecture. Current main `settlements.json` contains only `winterfell`; reference places are NOT automatically materialized NPC towns.

1. Author a **disjoint** resident census only after no duplicate location/settlement inheritance; a city's administrative district is included **inside**, not added to, its city total. Castle staff are part of households, not additional human populations.
2. Keep `homeSettlementId`, `currentPosition`, `militaryPostingId`, `legalDomicile`, `cultureOriginId`, `workplaceId` separate. A sailor arriving from Oldtown in King's Landing remains in Oldtown's resident count unless migration updates home.
3. Birth, age, fertility, marriage, deaths, migration, apprenticeship, inheritance, faith changes, feudal status and emancipation modify the authoritative people, with actual households. Work and military cannot create or clone “non-NPC” labor.
4. Food production and consumption share one population denominator. Full cohorts are compressed data; **materializing** 20 persons must reduce the cohort count by exactly 20, expanding them with identities/households. Abstracting them later must not produce simultaneous extra villagers.
5. Human physical spawn/culling does not change census; the visible entity is a **projection of an existing person** with known history, position, injuries, memories, law and social ties.
6. Preserve difference between **ruin dwellers**, **city workers**, **visiting merchants**, **captives**, **permanent fishers** and **marching armies**; all remain unique persons with persistent circumstances.

### 8.5 Farms, economic inventory and labor hours

Existing owners: `HoldingAgricultureCatalog`, `AgriculturalDayEnrollment`, `EconomyRuntime`, `InventoryManager`, `EconomicOwner`, resource definitions and economy journal/snapshot checkpoint services. `NpcActionProcessor.processWork` still has no output side effect; don't create a second arbitrary job-output authority.

1. Compute physical farmable/cultivated area at holding/settlement-region level with terrain class, river/well access, slopes and estate/property rights. Current `agricultural_capacity.json` has region profiles **only for `the_north`, `the_reach`, `dorne`**; SW Riverlands/West/Iron/Crownlands/Vale/Stormlands have no exact current region profile there. Add justified inputs, NOT pretend current placeholder records are real measured output.
2. Crops require seeds/soil/weather/season/water and **actual labor-days**, livestock require pasture/feed, fisheries real catch grounds and crews, mines ore veins/picks/fuel/miners and processing.
3. `WORK` NPC goals should dispatch **once** into existing economic transaction logic, with job/workplace/person/day IDs and anti-double-credit. Work hours used on ferries or levies cannot simultaneously be used harvesting fields.
4. Harvest to inventory; households consume from owned/received resources; spoilage, household hunger, health and death change actual NPCs. Long winters or sieges alter reserves, market prices and political legitimacy.
5. Markets move actual goods and currencies/tokens between owners, while taxes/tolls/wages/loans pay actual people and institutions. `taxBase` ≠ coin in lord's purse.
6. Estate fields, city land, owner dynasty and individual houses are separate property rights. A family can hold a castle while merchants control docks and independent smallholders retain separate plots.
7. Mines/farms need labor inputs beyond buildings: miners' injuries, fuel use, collapsed tunnels, timber depletion, water scarcity, drought, disease and weather should be simulation drivers only if corresponding physical context exists.
8. Example integrity invariant: `grainProductionKg <= min(cultivatedHectares * supportedYield, availableFarmerWorkDays * outputPerWorkDay)` plus seed/stock/crop reductions and weather.

### 8.6 Trade/transport network and scarcity propagation

Node types: active inland market town, capital city, riverport, safe harbor, mine, shipyard, ford, mountain gate, rural grain cluster, island, ferry, oasis, military depot. Each graph edge must have **distance, travel time, capacity, terrain/sea conditions, legal toll, risk, vessel/animal requirements**, and ability to fail or be blockaded.

Source-supported major SW edge families:
- Reach farms → Mander barges / Roseroad → Oldtown and King's Landing; vital capital food dependency.
- Redwyne Arbor vineyards/wineries → Ryamsport/Vinetown/Starfish Harbor → Oldtown/other ports, fleet escort and cargo.
- Westerlands mines → Casterly Rock/Lannisport or Golden Tooth/Goldroad → King’s Landing; precious metal not automatically spendable coin.
- Riverlands grains/cattle → barge Trident / Crossroads Inn / river road → towns, and army requisitions during future wars.
- Vale interior wheat/fruit → Bloody Gate/high road or Gulltown sea port, seasonally blocked; mountain clan risk varies.
- Iron Isles fishing/foundries → Lordsport and Harlaw/Pyke island market ports; cold maritime preservation and ship supply.
- Stormlands timber and rainwood food → Weeping Town/Stonehelm/Storm's End logistical routes; storms affect sailing.
- Dornish Greenblood orchard/farm output → Planky Town rafts/coastal vessels; desert oases and Boneway/Prince's Pass, water as major limiting commodity.
- Royal fleet in Blackwater Bay/The Gullet: all vessels have owner, ship class, full actual crews, supplies, repair, wages and date-scoped assignment.

Every traveling merchant is **already an NPC**; its cargo in `InventoryManager` leaves seller/store once and enters buyer/store once. Exchange can fail from storm or blockade, changing hunger and prices rather than silently teleporting grain across a sea.

### 8.7 Soldiers, logistics, war, courts and crime

1. Source military capacities are not standing armies. At day zero regions are not all mobilized. Garrisons + militia + levies + cavalry + Redwyne warship sailors + Iron Fleet crews require one military-person membership/available workday model.
2. Distinguish professional City Watch in King's Landing/Oldtown, noble retinues, marcher knights, ironborn ship crews, castle household guards, royal fleet and localized city/port watch. These have actual organization/titles and employers.
3. Multiple military organizations may claim the same commander, not multiple humans. Tax-financed budgets must purchase equipment/provisions from the actual economy.
4. Wars change supply, land control, property, household migration, disease/hunger, road safety, rumor spreading and historical memories; conflict outcomes must emerge from decisions, not scripted later books.
5. Courts and laws differ: criminal punishment by Crown institutions, feudal rights, local Vale clan law, Ironborn thrall/reaving customs and Dornish inheritance. Crimes affect real witnesses and reputations, including player actions.
6. Naval combat and blockade must operate on actual vessels with real sailor losses and stolen/consumed cargo; war mobilization removes farmer/miner/shipwright time and therefore decreases output.

### 8.8 Religions, cultural variation, families, knowledge and history

Current **verified** reference IDs:
`riverlander`, `westerman`, `ironborn`, `crownlander`, `valeman`, `vale_mountain_clan`, `sisterman`, `reachman`, `stormlander`, `dornish`, `stony_dornish`, `sandy_dornish`, `salty_dornish`, `orphan_greenblood` from `reference/cultures.json`. Religion IDs include `faith_of_the_seven`, `old_gods`, `drowned_god`, `rhllor`, `unknown_religion`, plus other global religions; don't invent IDs like `dornish_seven`.

- Assign distributions by region and **specific exceptions**: old gods Blackwoods in the Riverlands; Drowned God Ironborn; Dorne Rhoynish-descended communities; Vale mountain clans and Sistermen; capital cosmopolitan visitors and Oldtown foreign temples. Faith/culture distributions are *initial generator priors*, **not compulsory** individuals.
- House Hightower/Citadel: maester vows/education/institutions, apprenticeships and scholarly roles rather than extra government dynasty; old history of Starry Sept's national supremacy no longer true.
- Include known royal alliances/marriages and old rivalries (Blackwood-Bracken; Tyrell vs Florent/Gardener historical claim; Stark–Arryn–Tully ties; Lannister wealth/political strategy; Martell grievance after Robert's Rebellion; Greyjoy 289 rebellion; Connington exile; Reyne and Tarbeck destruction) without programming future choices as inevitable.
- Names/skills/personalities derive from existing character packs and regional influences, not stereotypes. Propagate knowledge through encounters, ravens, books, trade, observation and rumor, not omniscient access to hidden lore.

### 8.9 Temporal, save/restart and deployment acceptance

- Campaign starting `startYear=298`, `startDayOfYear=0` is **simulation-relative**; don't map to an invented in-world date. `seasonId=long_summer`. Before the Royal crypt conversation, Bran's fall and war; `Jon Arryn` already dead. [R]
- Later storyline events (Robert death, Joffrey accession, Harrenhal grants, Red Wedding, Doran moves to Water Gardens, Renly/Stannis rival kingships, Tyrell royal marriage, Ironborn kingsmoot) must be possible but **not pre-assigned events**; use event histories plus autonomous choices.
- Cross-system saves cover legal holding changes, persons, age, health, relationships, inventory, workplace contracts, farms, ships, armies, prices, roads and discovered map sites with consistent checkpoints.
- A physical build must be validated for topology on a 1:1 scale: river joins, bridges and nearby castle positions; don't place settlements under water or farmland inside cliff/sea pixels.
- Performance is tested for one city plus several farm villages and two market/trade routes over decades, then region and world. Players entering a market must meet the **same** NPCs previously simulated off-screen.

## 9. Specific Step 2.2 migration backlog — source evidence and intended owner

| Priority | Finding | Existing authority | Required change or verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | **198 SW records, but `settlements.json` still only Winterfell** | `LandedHoldingManager`, `SettlementManager` | Map legal holdings to actual settlement structures only after validation; do not auto-create one city per holding |
| P0 | **Oldtown has no `CITY` holding** though Hightower fortress does | `reference/locations.json`, `landed_holdings.json` | Author canonical `oldtown` settlement with Hightower/Citadel/sept subassets and actual inhabitants; optionally city holding only with political justification |
| P0 | King's Landing and Red Keep separate legal records | `kings_landing_domain`, `red_keep_domain` | One city/urban census; castle is child workplace and court, not additive population |
| P0 | Gulltown three holdings share `worldLocationId=gulltown` | Grafton, Arryn Gulltown, Shett Gulltown | Audit current legal rights; **one** geographically distinct city, not 3 populations or walls |
| P0 | Saltpans two town holdings share `worldLocationId=saltpans` | House Cox / House Hawick | Keep ownership ambiguity until source reconciliation; never automatically duplicate Saltpans |
| P0 | Harlaw, Orkmont, Old Wyk, Saltcliffe have overlapping multi-house land tenures | Iron Island families | Multi-parcel/property relationships under single island and shared ports/roads |
| P0 | `gray_shield` raster vs `grey_shield` geography spelling | `subregion_masks.json` / registry | Normalize one stable ID and aliases, test raster to actual feature location resolution |
| P0 | `the_prince_pass` raster vs `the_princes_pass` feature | Same | Normalize one pass ID and test region/routing priority |
| P0 | House hierarchy must respect nested vassals | `dynasty_hierarchy.json` | Validate Harlaw cadets, Sunderland subordinate houses, Vance branches, Frey sub-vassals, Goldengrove links |
| P0 | Main scenario only has 3 agriculture regions (North, Reach, Dorne) | `agricultural_capacity.json` | Validate fertility/season/source and add missing SW profiles; define zone-specific overrides rather than realm constant |
| P0 | Productive `WORK` still unimplemented | `NpcActionProcessor.processWork` | Wire real jobs to single economic output ledger with no duplicate labor or “ghost” workers |
| P0 | Resident/cohort architecture not authoritative at global scale | `NpcRegistry`, population models | Stable identity census, disjoint cohorts, real household job and military membership |
| P1 | Source ruins `the_whispers`, Oldstones/Castamere/Dragonpit require explicit physical status | WorldLocation, site status | Keep legal holding status distinct from ruined built fabric and current actual occupancy |
| P1 | 298 court actors currently physically visiting Winterfell | `scenario_start.json`, population packs | Same Robert/Cersei/Jaime/Tyrion person identities, no copies residing at King's Landing |
| P1 | Doran Water Gardens move happens later in 298 | Events/calendar + `water_gardens` | Keep exact chronology unresolved relative to early anchor; do not move prince on scene start |
| P1 | Planky Town floats in river; Shadow City next to Sunspear | World geometry and settlement archetypes | Special raft/river and adjacent mudbrick town generation; independent households, shared principality |
| P1 | Oldtown Citadel and Faith are separate organizations | Culture/religion/institutions/organization managers | Real officeholders/maesters/septons, training, economy and title independence |
| P1 | Ironborn fishing, ore, ship crews and captives | Inventory, professions, legal status | Correct resource/output constraints and actual person/ship ownership |
| P1 | Riverlands armies can burn villages | Economy, military, households, event log | Conflict damages real stocks/property and causes migration, not only `militaryValue` |
| P1 | Redwyne war/merchant fleet unusually large and source-backed | Ships / economy / orgs | Distinguish capacity/inventory from deployed crews, wages and supply; preserve fleet roster |
| P1 | Rich Reach valley vs desert Dorne and isolated islands | Zone/biome/agriculture | Physical land capability masks by altitude/hydrology/weather, not same province-wide yield |
| P1 | Border roads/fords/pass routes and ocean channels | Map geo / logistics graph | Edge cost/capacity and actual physical connections, no teleportation across forbidden mountains |
| P2 | Historical wars, extinct houses, monuments | Chronicle, claims, memory | Distinguish dated history from current active lords and modern occupied sites |
| P2 | Unnamed rural places, farms, industry and port wards | Population scale/cultural worldgen | Procedural non-canon names and buildings tied to land, working economy and residents |
| P2 | Map and detection ambiguity | Authoring reconciliation | Preserve raw `SW-NNN`, image pixels, aliases; defer false/missed human correction |

### 9.1 Acceptance tests (do not declare Step 2.2 implemented without them)

- [ ] Every source match / miss / false positive has a preserved raw marker ID; every accepted place joins existing reference or reviewed new ID.
- [ ] Exactly eight SW territorial domains and **198 current holding records** can be validated without destroying unrelated continents' holdings.
- [ ] Site and settlement cardinality is independent of holding records: `Gulltown=1 city`, `Saltpans=1 town`, `Pyke island != Pyke castle`, `Dragonstone island != Dragonstone castle`, `Oldtown city != Hightower tower`, `Sunspear palace != Shadow City != Planky Town`.
- [ ] All 73 South geography feature IDs resolvable; all 31 SW raster zone color IDs resolve or are explicitly corrected; region and sea/land masks agree.
- [ ] Foreign keys of all 298 AC owners/lieges/officeholders either reference canonical entities or are null with explicit audit.
- [ ] Each settlement has unique permanent resident census; `residentNpcs + unmaterializedCohortResidents = residentPopulation`, non-overlap enforced.
- [ ] City watch, household guard, levee/fleet sailors and employed workers are **subsets of resident or transient authoritative persons**, not extra people.
- [ ] Actual harvest depends on usable crop land + labor and actual stores; household food consumes exactly inventory once. No additional phantom `WORK` output.
- [ ] All castle/ruin physical generation uses valid land/terrain heights, unique canonical locations, road/river coast topologies.
- [ ] King Robert and royal party NPCs exist exactly once physically at Winterfell on the first scenario tick; still king over Crownlands while away.
- [ ] Hoster Tully alive, Shella Whent holds Harrenhal, Robert Arryn minor, Balon Greyjoy not Euron King, Renly Storm's End, Stannis Dragonstone, Doran Sunspear, Mace Highgarden.
- [ ] No later house dispossession/rape/assault/rebellion/siege is pre-applied as initial state.
- [ ] Full save/load/long-duration fast-forward keeps identical people, employment days, property and political ownership with no double-charging/negative stocks.

## 10. Global 2.1 / map-correction queue (explicitly **deferred per user**) 

The user has elected to handle unreadable, false, missed and mispositioned image X observations **after** all submap research, using saved Python output. Therefore no unverified `SW-001`, `SW-002`... fake detector coordinates occur here. `SW-H###` is a **holding audit index** only, and all source-marker fields remain nullable.

Open correction items include: handwritten SW-specific labels not identified via repository; precise locations of smaller estates; `pyke` castle vs island; `vaith` river vs castle; `oldtown` city vs Hightower; island mask colors; `the_prince_pass` vs `the_princes_pass`; `gray_shield` vs `grey_shield`; overlapping NW `the_twins`, `the_fingers`, `the_neck` and `cape_kraken`; House Cox/Hawick Saltpans conflicting property; Gulltown multi-house ownership; historically ruined sites with `ACTIVE` holding statuses; source-book-only vs game-adaptation seat provenance; holding-specific crop yields and population ranges.

Proposed global row: `rawImageSha | mapId | rawObservationId | originalPixelX | originalPixelY | handwrittenText | candidateLocationId | status | source | crossmapDuplicateIds | reviewReason | userCorrectedPixel | masterCoordinate | worldXZ | acceptedCanonicalSiteId | implementationState`.

**Crucial Step 2.1 caveat:** This file researches all identified **catalogued holdings and feature-registry sites** plus important book-supported site additions. It cannot honestly assert every visible X is matched without pixel access. It is an extensive **research baseline and integration inventory**, not a definitive map-coordinate census. The NW file also warrants a later source-rich expansion rather than being declared sufficient merely because both regional docs now exist.

## 11. Source index and provenance for authoring

### Book-continuity geographical/historical indexing (secondary source pages linked to published books/maps)

- **[S01]** [Riverlands](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Riverlands) — Trident, river transport, towns and houses; based on *The World of Ice & Fire* and main novels.
- **[S02]** [Westerlands](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Westerlands) — castles, mines, coastline and roads; underlying *The World of Ice & Fire*, *The Lands of Ice and Fire*.
- **[S03]** [Iron Islands](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Iron_Islands) — island economy, fisheries, iron, reaving legal history; underlying books and historical maester records.
- **[S04]** [Vale of Arryn](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Vale_of_Arryn) — fertile interior, mountain clans, trade routes, vassals, Gulltown and Eyrie.
- **[S05]** [Crownlands](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Crownlands) / [King's Landing](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/King%27s_Landing) — Crownlands places, population hierarchy, capital institutions/harbor.
- **[S06]** [Reach](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Reach) / [Highgarden](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Highgarden) — fertile crops, houses, roads, river and governance.
- **[S07]** [Oldtown](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Oldtown) / [Citadel](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Citadel) — city, Hightower, academia, Starry Sept and trade.
- **[S08]** [House Tyrell](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/House_Tyrell) — succession/family, Reach institutions, future historic (not scenario-start) narrative.
- **[S09]** [The Arbor](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Arbor) / [Redwyne fleet](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Redwyne_fleet) — ports, wine, Paxter's ships and commerce.
- **[S10]** [Stormlands](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Stormlands) / [Storm's End](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Storm%27s_End) / [Cape Wrath](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Cape_Wrath) / [Dornish Marches](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Dornish_Marches).
- **[S11]** [Dorne](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Dorne) / [Greenblood](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Greenblood) / [Planky Town](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Planky_Town) / [Sunspear](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Sunspear) / [Water Gardens](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Water_Gardens).
- **[S12]** George R. R. Martin, *A Game of Thrones*, especially opening Winterfell scenes; *A Clash of Kings*, *A Storm of Swords*, *A Feast for Crows*, *A Dance with Dragons* for historical context, NOT premature future events.
- **[S13]** G. R. R. Martin / Elio M. García Jr / Linda Antonsson, *The World of Ice & Fire*; official *The Lands of Ice and Fire* (canonical cartography); *Fire & Blood* and *The Knight of the Seven Kingdoms* for strictly marked historical context.
- **[S14]** [Hoster Tully](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Hoster_Tully) and [Harrenhal](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Harrenhal) — holder chronology; Shella Whent early 298, later transfer only through events.
- **[S15]** [House Greyjoy](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/House_Greyjoy) / [Pyke](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Pyke) — Balon's lordship, 289 rebellion, Theon wardship and old institutions. Beware current-character infoboxes on wiki may display **later-book** incumbent.
- **[S16]** [House Baratheon of Dragonstone](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/House_Baratheon_of_Dragonstone) / [Lord of Dragonstone](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Lord_of_Dragonstone) — Stannis appointment after rebellion, separate from Renly.
- **[S17]** [Lannisport](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Lannisport) / [Casterly Rock](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Casterly_Rock) — separate castle and port economies.
- **[S18]** [Riverrun](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Riverrun) / [The Twins](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Twins) / [Maidenpool](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/Maidenpool) — distinctive construction, ownership and urban categories.
- **[S19]** [298 AC](https://awoiaf.westeros.org/index.php/298_AC) chronology index, alongside the repository's **more precise** `scenario_start.json` event anchor.

A Wiki of Ice and Fire is a **secondary index** to primary-book chapters and atlas entries. Statements about exact 298 date, specific minor lord and precise crop mix must not be assumed proven merely because the generic regional page exists. All such particulars are `[I]`, `[M]`, `[R]` or `[U]` until better sourced.
